-0,0 +1,8 @@\n+# C11: site-local realization of the BKSF codes\n+\n+- `siteaction.py`: read naively as three axis qubits per site (site v owns its edges (v,+a)), the BKSF codes\n+  are almost never symmetric under the framework's site rotation: 57 codes have a trivial stabilizer and 3\n+  have a C3. The reason is that the site rotation maps reversed edges to the wrong site.\n+- `halfedge.py`: use six direction-labelled qubits per site (half-edges) with a Z Z repetition stabilizer\n+  on each edge. The site action then maps the edge operators exactly, the stabilizers equal the edge-action\n+  stabilizers, and the orbit sizes are 4/6/12 (now in the runner)."},{"sha":"9c0d10e433fe239598ef273b94e180df3512ecd6","filename":".claude/science/physics-loops/toe-campaign-20261017/c11_site_local/halfedge.py","status":"added","additions":81,"deletions":0,"changes":81,"blob_url":"https://github.com/jonathonreilly/qubit-lattice-axiom-framework/blob/1a3546f6d63ba2728ff5010f1681a7c9df13bc10/.claude%2Fscience%2Fphysics-loops%2Ftoe-campaign-20261017%2Fc11_site_local%2Fhalfedge.py","raw_url":"https://github.com/jonathonreilly/qubit-lattice-axiom-framework/raw/1a3546f6d63ba2728ff5010f1681a7c9df13bc10/.claude%2Fscience%2Fphysics-loops%2Ftoe-campaign-20261017%2Fc11_site_local%2Fhalfedge.py","contents_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/jonathonreilly/qubit-lattice-axiom-framework/contents/.claude%2Fscience%2Fphysics-loops%2Ftoe-campaign-20261017%2Fc11_site_local%2Fhalfedge.py?ref=1a3546f6d63ba2728ff5010f1681a7c9df13bc10","patch":"@@ -0,0 +1,81 @@\n+\"\"\"BKSF codes with six direction-labelled qubits per site (half-edges): site-local, translation- and\n+site-rotation covariant realization. Z2) for G = Z2xZ2 and G = S3 (=D3) by enumerating normalized\n+2-cocycles modulo coboundaries; test whether the class is determined by the\n+squares w(g,g) of the involutions, and compute the class of the SU(2)\n+restriction (w2) on the cubic D2 {1, C2z, C2_(110), C2_(1-10)} and D3.\"\"\"\n+import itertools\n+import numpy as np\n+\n+def cohomology(elems, mul, inv_list):\n+    n = len(elems); e = 0\n+    pairs = [(a, b) for a in range(n) for b in range(n)]\n+    # cocycle condition w(a,b)+w(ab,c) = w(b,c)+w(a,bc) (mod 2), normalized w(e,.)=w(.,e)=0\n+    free = [(a, b) for (a, b) in pairs if a != e and b != e]\n+    cocycles = []\n+    for bits in itertools.product([0, 1], repeat=len(free)):\n+        w = {p: 0 for p in pairs}\n+        for p, v in zip(free, bits): w[p] = v\n+        if all((w[(a, b)] + w[(mul[a][b], c)] + w[(b, c)] + w[(a, mul[b][c])]) % 2 == 0\n+               for a in range(n) for b in range(n) for c in range(n)):\n+            cocycles.append(w)\n+    cob = set()\n+    for nu in itertools.product([0, 1], repeat=n - 1):\n+        nuf = (0,) + nu\n+        cob.add(tuple((nuf[a] + nuf[b] + nuf[mul[a][b]]) % 2 for (a, b) in pairs))\n+    classes = {}\n+    for w in cocycles:\n+        key = min(tuple((w[p] + c[i]) % 2 for i, p in enumerate(pairs)) for c in cob)\n+        classes.setdefault(key, w)\n+    return classes, pairs\n+\n+def mats_group(gens):\n+    G = [np.eye(3, dtype=int)]\n+    changed = True\n+    while changed:\n+        changed = False\n+        for A in list(G):\n+            for g in gens:\n+                B = A @ g\n+                if not any((B == C).all() for C in G):\n+                    G.append(B); if it is in +-G (local,\n+contractible), lambda = sign of O in the signed group G_s.\n+\n+Contamination probe: lambda is computed for MANY random solutions (particular\n+solution + random nullspace vectors), so any dependence on the choice of S\n+(e.g. through extra symmetric local excitations) shows up as a spread.\n+\n+Usage: python3 general_pair_test.py L [quick]\n+\"\"\"\n+import itertools\n+import json\n+import random\n+import sys\n+import time\n+from collections import Counter\n+\n+from pauli_bksf import (Code, Lattice, Span, edge_perm, matvec, order_of,\n+                        permute_pauli, pmul, rotations, sign_vs_canonical, DIRS)\n+from pair_test import ToricCode, dagger, axis_type\n+\n+L = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 8\n+QUICK = len(sys.argv) > 2\n+lat = Lattice(L)\n+random.seed(7)\n+t0 = time.time()\n+\n+\n+def tdiff(a, b):\n+    return tuple(((a[i] - b[i] + L // 2) % L) - L // 2 for i in range(3))\n+\n+\n+def dinf(a, b):\n+    return max(abs(c) for c in tdiff(a, b))\n+\n+\n+def mpath(x, y):\n+    pts = [tuple(c % L for c in x)]\n+    cur = list(x)\n+    d = tdiff(y, x)\n+    for a in range(3):\n+        st = 1 if d[a] > 0 else -1\n+        for _ in range(abs(d[a])):\n+            cur[a] += st\n+            pts.append(tuple(c % L for c in cur))\n+    return pts\n+\n+\n+def solve_gf2(rows, ncols):\n+    \"\"\"rows: list of ints with bit ncols = rhs.     rr = r\n+        while True:\n+            low = rr & ((1 << ncols) - 1)\n+            if low == 0:\n+                break\n+            c = low.bit_length() - 1\n+            if c in piv:\n+ for an ordering of the six directions (the same at every site), generators B_v = Z on site v's six qubits, R_(v,a) = Z_(v,+a) Z_(v+e_a,-a), and plaquettes S_p = product of the four nearest-neighbour operators A_(v,a) = X_(v,+a) X_(v+e_a,-a) times Z on v's qubits ordered before +a times Z on (v+e_a)'s qubits ordered before -a. Exact: on the R code space this is the edge BKSF code of the companion notes (each edge carries one logical qubit, X = X X, Z = Z on either half), and the framework site rotation (permute sites and permute each site's six qubits as directions, Pauli labels unchanged) maps A_(v,a) of ordering o exactly to A of the rotated edge with the rotated ordering, and B and R to B and R. Checked (L = 4, all 60 codes; L = 6, one per orbit type): commuting, log2 code dimension 3, and the stabilizer of every code in O under the site action equals its stabilizer under the edge action (orbits 4, 6, 12). Consequently the statements of the BKSF orbit note and the bare-permutation note (fermionic charge, minimal orbit four, the spin-1/2 class under bare rotations) hold verbatim for this site-local model with the bare site action. Not claimed: other site-local encodings, star-supported generators (the plaquette generators span four sites), dynamics, or any physical identification.\"\n+upstream_dependencies:\n+  - bksf_cubic_fermion_codes_are_not_rotation_invariant_minimal_orbit_four_bounded_theorem_note_2026-10-17\n+  - bksf_cubic_fermion_codes_bare_permutation_rotations_act_spinorially_on_the_fermion_bounded_theorem_note_2026-10-17\n+runner: scripts/bksf_cubic_fermion_codes_site_local_six_direction_qubit_realization_2026_10_17.py\n+---\n+\n+# BKSF cubic fermion codes: a site-local realization with six direction qubits per site\n+\n+**Date:** 2026-10-17\n+**Type:** bounded_theorem\n+**Status:** bounded support, unaudited. Exact for the stated encoding.\n+Nothing is adopted.\n+\n+## Question\n+\n+The two BKSF notes of this campaign put one qubit on each edge of Z^3 and\n+rotate edges about a vertex. They flag this as outside the framework's\n+site-local possibility domain: the Qubit axiom attaches qubits to sites,\n+and the Lattice axiom rotates about sites.\n+\n+Can the same codes be realized site-locally, so that the framework's own\n+site rotation reproduces the edge rotation exactly?\n+\n+## Result\n+\n+1. **Encoding.** Give each site six qubits, one per direction (half-edges).\n+   - The pair stabilizer R_(v,a) = Z_(v,+a) Z_(v+e_a,-a) makes each edge\n+     carry one logical qubit, with X = X X and Z = Z on either half.\n+   - The BKSF operators become:\n+     - B_v = Z on site v's six qubits: one site;\n+     - the hop A_(v,a) = X_(v,+a) X_(v+e_a,-a) times Z on v's qubits\n+       ordered before +a, times Z on (v+e_a)'s qubits ordered before -a: a\n+       nearest-neighbour pair;\n+     - S_p, the product of the four A around a plaquette: four sites.\n+   - On the R code space, this is the edge code.\n+2. **Exact covariance.** The framework site rotation permutes the sites\n+   and permutes each site's six qubits as directions; Pauli labels are\n+   unchanged.\n+   - It maps A_(v,a) of ordering o exactly to A of the rotated edge with\n+     the rotated ordering.\n+   - It maps B to B and R to R.\n+   - So the encoding intertwines the site action with the edge action.\n+3. **Checks (runner).** All 60 codes, L = 4:\n+   - commuting, with log2 code dimension 3 (also 3 at L = 6 for one code\n+     of each orbit type);\n+   - each code's stabilizer in O under the site action equals its\n+     stabilizer under the edge action;\n+   - orbit sizes 4, 6 and 12.\n+4. **Transfer.** The encoding is an isometry that intertwines the two\n+   actions. So the companion notes' statements hold verbatim for this\n+   site-local model with the bare site action:\n+   - a fermionic point charge;\n+   - no O-invariant code, with minimal orbit four;\n+   - the spin-1/2 fractionalization class under bare rotations.\n+\n+## How this bears on the lane (interpretation, not a claim)\n+\n+- **The edge caveat is removed.** The emergent fermion of these notes now\n+  lives in a model with qubits on the framework's sites. The framework's\n+  own rotation is a bare permutation of sites and of each site's\n+  direction-labelled qubits.\n+- **Two extensions remain.**\n+  - Six qubits per site is an enlargement of the Qubit axiom, as the three\n+    axis qubits of the companion notes are.\n+  - The plaquette generators span four sites, beyond the nearest-neighbour\n+    star.\n+- **Consistent with the earlier no-gos.** The campaign's star-supported\n+  searches found no coherently mobile fermion. Here the fermion appears\n+  once the conditions include plaquettes, a slightly larger neighbourhood.\n+\n+## Premises\n+\n+- **Axioms used.** Lattice (Z^3, unit translations, proper cubic rotations\n+  about sites).\n+- **Supplied model.** The encoding above. Qubit is read as six\n+  direction-labelled qubits per site, a declared enlargement.\n+- **Declared choices.** The same ordering at every site; unsoldered Pauli\n+  labels.\n+\n+## Relation to prior work\n+\n+- BKSF: Bravyi and Kitaev, Ann. Phys. 298, 210 (2002).\n+- Generalized superfast encodings with qubits attached to vertices: Setia,\n+  Bravyi, Mezzacapo and Whitfield, PRR 1, 033033 (2019). The repetition\n+  encoding of edge qubits into half-edges is elementary.\n+- **New here.** The observation that the framework's site rotation acts\n+  exactly as the edge rotation for this encoding, so that the campaign's\n+  BKSF results transfer to a site-local model.\n+\n+## No-Go Discipline Gate\n+\n+- **N1 — routes around the claim.** Other site-local encodings, such as\n+  the generalized superfast encoding with three qubits per site, may have\n+  different symmetry. They are not treated.\n+- **N2/N3 — scope.** One encoding of one family.\n+- **N4 — inputs.** Exact GF(2) checks. The transfer in item 4 is the\n+  intertwining identity of item 2.\n+- **N5 — wording.** \"Site-local\" means qubits on sites. It does not mean\n+  star-supported generators.\n+- **N6/N7 — strongest objection.** The six qubits per site and the\n+  plaquette range are enlargements. Both are stated.\n+- **N8 — earlier claims.** Removes the \"qubits on edges, not sites\" caveat\n+  of the two BKSF notes. Nothing else changes.\n+\n+## Verification\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 scripts/bksf_cubic_fermion_codes_site_local_six_direction_qubit_realization_2026_10_17.py\n+```\n+\n+Expected: `TOTAL: PASS=6 FAIL=0` -0,0 +1,185 @@\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\n+\"\"\"Site-local realization of the translation-invariant BKSF cubic fermion codes.\n+\n+Six qubits per site of Z^3, one per direction d in {+x,+y,+z,-x,-y,-z} (half-edges). For an ordering of the six\n+directions (the same at every site):\n+  B_v       = Z on the six qubits of site v                           (one site)\n+  R_(v,a)   = Z_(v,+a) Z_(v+e_a,-a)                                   (nearest-neighbour pair; repetition)\n+  A_(v,a)   = X_(v,+a) X_(v+e_a,-a) * Z on v's qubits ordered before +a * Z on (v+e_a)'s qubits ordered before -a\n+                                                                      (nearest-neighbour pair; charge hop)\n+  S_p       = product of the four A around a plaquette                 (four sites of a plaquette)\n+Code = <B, R, S>. On the repetition code space each edge carries one logical qubit (X = X X, Z = Z on either\n+half) and the code is the edge BKSF code of the companion notes.\n+\n+The framework site rotation about a site (permute sites, and permute each site's six qubits as directions)\n+induces exactly the edge rotation on the logical qubits. The runner checks, on the L = 4 torus:\n+- all 60 codes (classes of the 720 orderings) are commuting with log2 code dimension 3 (also L = 6, one code\n+  per orbit type);\n+- the site action maps A_(v,a) of ordering o exactly to A of the rotated edge with the rotated ordering;\n+- the stabilizer of each code in O under the site action equals its stabilizer under the edge action, so the\n+  orbits have sizes 4, 6 and 12 (12, 12 and 36 codes), as for the edge codes.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import itertools\n+import os\n+import sys\n+from collections import Counter, defaultdict\n+\n+import numpy as np\n+\n+sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\n+import bksf_cubic_fermion_codes_rotation_orbits_2026_10_17 as bk  # noqa: E402\n+import cubic_covariant_nearest_neighbour_stabilizer_rules_one_qubit_defects_2026_10_17 as c1  # noqa: E402\n+\n+AUDIT_TIMEOUT_SEC = 1200\n+AUDIT_INPUT_PATHS = (\n+    'docs/BKSF_CUBIC_FERMION_CODES_SITE_LOCAL_REALIZATION_WITH_SIX_DIRECTION_QUBITS_PER_SITE_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-10-17.md',\n+)\n+\n+E, DIRS = bk.E, bk.DIRS\n+\n+\n+class Half:\n+    def __init__(self, L):\n+        self.L = L\n+        self.n = 6 * L ** 3\n+        self.V = list(itertools.product(range(L), repeat=3))\n+\n+    def q(self, v, d):\n+        L = self.L\n+        return 6 * (((v[0] % L) * L + (v[1] % L)) * L + (v[2] % L)) + d\n+\n+    def A(self, v, a, order):\n+        x = np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8)\n+        z = np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8)\n+        w = tuple(v[i] + E[a][i] for i in range(3))\n+        dp, dm = DIRS.index((a, 1)), DIRS.index((a, -1))\n+        x[self.q(v, dp)] ^= 1\n+        x[self.q(w, dm)] ^= 1\n+        pos = {d: i for i, d in enumerate(order)}\n+        for d in range(6):\n+            if pos[d] < pos[dp]:\n+                z[self.q(v, d)] ^= 1\n+            if pos[d] < pos[dm]:\n+                z[self.q(w, d)] ^= 1\n+        return np.concatenate([x, z])\n+\n+    def B(self, v):\n+        z = np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8)\n+        for d in range(6):\n+            z[self.q(v, d)] = 1\n+        return np.concatenate([np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8), z])\n+\n+    def R(self, v, a):\n+        z = np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8)\n+        w = tuple(v[i] + E[a][i] for i in range(3))\n+        z[self.q(v, DIRS.index((a, 1)))] = 1\n+        z[self.q(w, DIRS.index((a, -1)))] = 1\n+        return np.concatenate([np.zeros(self.n, np.uint8), z])\n+\n+    def S(self, v, c, order):\n+        a, b = [t for t in range(3) if t != c]\n+        va = tuple(v[i] + E[a][i] for i in range(3))\n+        vb = tuple(v[i] + E[b][i] for i in range(3))\n+        return self.A(v, a, order) ^ self.A(va, b, order) ^ self.A(vb, a, order) ^ self.A(v, b, order)\n+\n+    def code(self, order):\n+        return np.array([self.B(v) for v in self.V] + [self.R(v, a) for v in self.V for a in range(3)]\n+                        + [self.S(v, c, order) for v in self.V for c in range(3)])\n+\n+    def site_perm(self, M):\n+        perm = np.zeros(self.n, int)\n+        for v in self.V:\n+            w = tuple(int(x) for x in M @ np.array(v))\n+            for d in range(6):\n+                a, s = DIRS[d]\n+                dv = M @ (s * np.array(E[a]))\n+                b = int(np.nonzero(dv)[0][0])\n+                perm[self.q(v, d)] = self.q(w, DIRS.index((b, int(dv[b]))))\n+        return perm\n+\n+    def act(self, R, perm):\n+        out = np.zeros_like(R)\n+        out[:, perm] = R[:, :self.n]\n+        out[:, self.n + perm] = R[:, self.n:]\n+        return out\n+\n+    def commuting(self, C):\n+        n = self.n\n+        return not ((C[:, :n].astype(np.int32) @ C[:, n:].T.astype(np.int32)\n+                     + C[:, n:].astype(np.int32) @ C[:, :n].T.astype(np.int32)) % 2).any()\n+\n+\n+def rank(M):\n+    A = (np.array(M, np.uint8) % 2).copy()\n+    m, n = A.shape\n+    r = 0\n+    for c in range(n):\n+        nz = np.flatnonzero(A[r:, c])\n+        if nz.size == 0:\n+            continue\n+        p = r + nz[0]\n+        if p != r:\n+            A[[r, p]] = A[[p, r]]\n+        rows = np.flatnonzero(A[:, c])\n+        rows = rows[rows != r]\n+        if rows.size:\n+            A[rows] ^= A[r]\n+        r += 1\n+        if r == m:\n+            break\n+    return r\n+\n+\n+def main() -> int:\n+    ck = c1.Checks()\n+    orders = list(itertools.permutations(range(6)))\n+    Hedge = {o: bk.span_hash(bk.torus_rows(bk.gens(o), 4)) for o in orders}\n+    classes = base = (0, 0, 0) if d[b] == 1 else tuple(-E[b][t] for t in range(3))\n+                exact &= bool((img == h4.A(base, b, bk.rot_order(M, o))).all())\n+    ck.check('all 60 half-edge codes are commuting stabilizer groups (L = 4)', comm)\n+    ck.check('all 60 have log2 code dimension 3 on the L = 4 torus', dims == Counter({3: 60}))\n+    ck.check('the site action (permute sites and the six direction labels) maps A_(v,a) of ordering o exactly to A of the rotated edge with the rotated ordering (all 24 rotations, all 60 codes)', exact)\n+    ck.check('for every code, its stabilizer in O under the site action equals its stabilizer under the edge action', same_stab)\n+    print('orbit sizes under the site action (size: number of codes):', dict(orb))\n+    ck.check('orbits under the site action have sizes 4, 6 and 12 (12, 12 and 36 codes); no code is O-invariant', orb == Counter({4: 12, 6: 12, 12: 36}))\n+    h6 = Half(6)\n+    d6 = {k: h6.n - rank(h6.code(o)) for k, o in    print('per_element: all 60 codes; all 24 rotations')\n+    print('per_site: six qubits per site (one per direction); generators on one site, nearest-neighbour pairs and plaquettes')\n+    print('per_mode: not applicable')\n+    print('per_block: not applicable')\n+    print('lattice_wide: L = 4 torus (all codes), L = 6 (one per orbit type)')\n+    print(f\"TOTAL: PASS={ck.passed}
